Next.js application</h1><div class="text-sm text-gray-900 mt-2 mb-6">Last updated <!-- -->August 25, 2026</div>
<p>When <a href="/docs/app/getting-started/deploying">deploying</a> your Next.js app, you may want to configure how different features are handled based on your infrastructure.</p>
<blockquote class="p-3 text-sm">
<p><strong>🎥 Watch:</strong> Learn more about self-hosting Next.js → <a href="https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=sIVL4JMqRfc" rel="noopener noreferrer nofollow" target="_blank">YouTube (45 minutes)<span class="inline-flex not-prose"><svg viewBox="0 0 16 16" height="16" width="16" data-slot="geist-icon" style="color:currentColor"><path fill="currentColor" fill-rule="evenodd" d="M6.75 4H6v1.5h3.44L5.47 9.47l-.53.53L6 11.06l.53-.53 3.97-3.97V10H12V5a1 1 0 0 0-1-1z" clip-rule="evenodd"></path></svg></span></a>.</p>
</blockquote>
<h2 id="reverse-proxy" data-docs-heading="">Reverse Proxy<a aria-label="Link to this section" data-heading-link="" href="#reverse-proxy"><span><svg viewBox="0 0 16 16" height="0.7em" width="0.7em"><g stroke-width="1.2" fill="none" stroke="currentColor"><path fill="none" stroke-linecap="round" stroke-linejoin="round" stroke-miterlimit="10" d="M8.995,7.005 L8.995,7.005c1.374,1.374,1.374,3.601,0,4.975l-1.99,1.99c-1.374,1.374-3.601,1.374-4.975,0l0,0c-1.374-1.374-1.374-3.601,0-4.975 l1.748-1.698"></path><path fill="none" stroke-linecap="round" stroke-linejoin="round" stroke-miterlimit="10" d="M7.005,8.995 L7.005,8.995c-1.374-1.374-1.374-3.601,0-4.975l1.99-1.99c1.374-1.374,3.601-1.374,4.975,0l0,0c1.374,1.374,1.374,3.601,0,4.975 l-1.748,1.698"></path></g></svg></span></a></h2>
<p>When self-hosting, it&#x27;s recommended to use a reverse proxy (like nginx) in front of your Next.js server rather than exposing it directly to the internet. A reverse proxy can handle malformed requests, slow connection attacks, payload size limits, rate limiting, and other security concerns, offloading these tasks from the Next.js server. This allows the server to dedicate its resources to rendering rather than request validation.</p>
<h2 id="image-optimization" data-docs-heading="">Image Optimization<a aria-label="Link to this section" data-heading-link="" href="#image-optimization"><span><svg viewBox="0 0 16 16" height="0.7em" width="0.7em"><g stroke-width="1.2" fill="none" stroke="currentColor"><path fill="none" stroke-linecap="round" stroke-linejoin="round" stroke-miterlimit="10" d="M8.995,7.005 L8.995,7.005c1.374,1.374,1.374,3.601,0,4.975l-1.99,1.99c-1.374,1.374-3.601,1.374-4.975,0l0,0c-1.374-1.374-1.374-3.601,0-4.975 l1.748-1.698"></path><path fill="none" stroke-linecap="round" stroke-linejoin="round" stroke-miterlimit="10" d="M7.005,8.995 L7.005,8.995c-1.374-1.374-1.374-3.601,0-4.975l1.99-1.99c1.374-1.374,3.601-1.374,4.975,0l0,0c1.374,1.374,1.374,3.601,0,4.975 l-1.748,1.698"></path></g></svg></span></a></h2>
<p><a href="/docs/app/api-reference/components/image">Image Optimization</a> through <code>next/image</code> works self-hosted with zero configuration when deploying using <code>next start</code>. If you would prefer to have a separate service to optimize images, you can <a href="/docs/app/api-reference/components/image#loader">configure an image loader</a>.</p>
<p>Image Optimization can be used with a <a href="/docs/app/guides/static-exports#image-optimization">static export</a> by defining a custom image loader in <code>next.config.js</code>. Note that images are optimized at runtime, not during the build.</p>
<blockquote class="p-3 text-sm">
<p><strong>Good to know:</strong></p>
<ul>
<li>On glibc-based Linux systems, Image Optimization may require <a href="https://sharp.pixelplumbing.com/install#linux-memory-allocator" rel="noopener noreferrer nofollow" target="_blank">additional configuration<span class="inline-flex not-prose"><svg viewBox="0 0 16 16" height="16" width="16" data-slot="geist-icon" style="color:currentColor"><path fill="currentColor" fill-rule="evenodd" d="M6.75 4H6v1.5h3.44L5.47 9.47l-.53.53L6 11.06l.53-.53 3.97-3.97V10H12V5a1 1 0 0 0-1-1z" clip-rule="evenodd"></path></svg></span></a> to prevent excessive memory usage.</li>
<li>Learn more about the <a href="/docs/app/api-reference/components/image#minimumcachettl">caching behavior of optimized images</a> and how to configure the TTL.</li>
<li>You can also <a href="/docs/app/api-reference/components/image#unoptimized">disable Image Optimization</a> and still retain other benefits of using <code>next/image</code> if you prefer. For example, if you are optimizing images yourself separately.</li>
</ul>
</blockquote>
<h2 id="proxy" data-docs-heading="">Proxy<a aria-label="Link to this section" data-heading-link="" href="#proxy"><span><svg viewBox="0 0 16 16" height="0.7em" width="0.7em"><g stroke-width="1.2" fill="none" stroke="currentColor"><path fill="none" stroke-linecap="round" stroke-linejoin="round" stroke-miterlimit="10" d="M8.995,7.005 L8.995,7.005c1.374,1.374,1.374,3.601,0,4.975l-1.99,1.99c-1.374,1.374-3.601,1.374-4.975,0l0,0c-1.374-1.374-1.374-3.601,0-4.975 l1.748-1.698"></path><path fill="none" stroke-linecap="round" stroke-linejoin="round" stroke-miterlimit="10" d="M7.005,8.995 L7.005,8.995c-1.374-1.374-1.374-3.601,0-4.975l1.99-1.99c1.374-1.374,3.601-1.374,4.975,0l0,0c1.374,1.374,1.374,3.601,0,4.975 l-1.748,1.698"></path></g></svg></span></a></h2>
<p><a href="/docs/app/api-reference/file-conventions/proxy">Proxy</a> works self-hosted with zero configuration when deploying using <code>next start</code>. Since it requires access to the incoming request, it is not supported when using a <a href="/docs/app/guides/static-exports">static export</a>.</p>
<p>If you are looking to add logic (or use an external package) that requires all Node.js APIs, you might be able to move this logic to a <a href="/docs/app/api-reference/file-conventions/layout">layout</a> as a <a href="/docs/app/getting-started/server-and-client-components">Server Component</a>. For example, checking <a href="/docs/app/api-reference/functions/headers">headers</a> and <a href="/docs/app/api-reference/functions/redirect">redirecting</a>. You can also use headers, cookies, or query parameters to <a href="/docs/app/api-reference/config/next-config-js/redirects#header-cookie-and-query-matching">redirect</a> or <a href="/docs/app/api-reference/config/next-config-js/rewrites#header-cookie-and-query-matching">rewrite</a> through <code>next.config.js</code>. If that does not work, you can also use a <a href="/docs/pages/guides/custom-server">custom server</a>.</p>
<h2 id="environment-variables" data-docs-heading="">Environment Variables<a aria-label="Link to this section" data-heading-link="" href="#environment-variables"><span><svg viewBox="0 0 16 16" height="0.7em" width="0.7em"><g stroke-width="1.2" fill="none" stroke="currentColor"><path fill="none" stroke-linecap="round" stroke-linejoin="round" stroke-miterlimit="10" d="M8.995,7.005 L8.995,7.005c1.374,1.374,1.374,3.601,0,4.975l-1.99,1.99c-1.374,1.374-3.601,1.374-4.975,0l0,0c-1.374-1.374-1.374-3.601,0-4.975 l1.748-1.698"></path><path fill="none" stroke-linecap="round" stroke-linejoin="round" stroke-miterlimit="10" d="M7.005,8.995 L7.005,8.995c-1.374-1.374-1.374-3.601,0-4.975l1.99-1.99c1.374-1.374,3.601-1.374,4.975,0l0,0c1.374,1.374,1.374,3.601,0,4.975 l-1.748,1.698"></path></g></svg></span></a></h2>
<p>Next.js can support both build time and runtime environment variables.</p>
<p><strong>By default, environment variables are only available on the server</strong>. To expose an environment variable to the browser, it must be prefixed with <code>NEXT_PUBLIC_</code>. However, these public environment variables will be inlined into the JavaScript bundle during <code>next build</code>.</p>
<!-- -->

<p>This allows you to use a singular Docker image that can be promoted through multiple environments with different values.</p>
<blockquote class="p-3 text-sm">
<p><strong>Good to know:</strong></p>
<ul>
<li>You can run code on server startup using the <a href="/docs/app/guides/instrumentation"><code>register</code> function</a>.</li>
</ul>
</blockquote>
<h2 id="caching-and-isr" data-docs-heading="">Caching and ISR<a aria-label="Link to this section" data-heading-link="" href="#caching-and-isr"><span><svg viewBox="0 0 16 16" height="0.7em" width="0.7em"><g stroke-width="1.2" fill="none" stroke="currentColor"><path fill="none" stroke-linecap="round" stroke-linejoin="round" stroke-miterlimit="10" d="M8.995,7.005 L8.995,7.005c1.374,1.374,1.374,3.601,0,4.975l-1.99,1.99c-1.374,1.374-3.601,1.374-4.975,0l0,0c-1.374-1.374-1.374-3.601,0-4.975 l1.748-1.698"></path><path fill="none" stroke-linecap="round" stroke-linejoin="round" stroke-miterlimit="10" d="M7.005,8.995 L7.005,8.995c-1.374-1.374-1.374-3.601,0-4.975l1.99-1.99c1.374-1.374,3.601-1.374,4.975,0l0,0c1.374,1.374,1.374,3.601,0,4.975 l-1.748,1.698"></path></g></svg></span></a></h2>
<p>Next.js can cache responses, generated static pages, build outputs, and other static assets like images, fonts, and scripts.</p>
<p>Caching and revalidating pages (with <a href="/docs/app/guides/incremental-static-regeneration">Incremental Static Regeneration</a>) use the <strong>same Next.js server cache</strong>. By default, this cache is stored on the local filesystem (on disk) of each Next.js server instance.</p>
<p>This works automatically for a single self-hosted <code>next start</code> instance with persistent local disk. If you run multiple instances, use ephemeral compute, or place a CDN/reverse proxy in front of Next.js, also review <a href="#configuring-caching">Configuring Caching</a>, <a href="#multi-instance-cache-coordination">Multi-Instance Cache Coordination</a>, and <a href="#usage-with-cdns">Usage with CDNs</a>.</p>
<p>You can configure the Next.js cache location if you want to persist cached pages and data to durable storage, or share the cache across multiple containers or instances of your Next.js application.</p>
<h3 id="automatic-caching" data-docs-heading="">Automatic Caching<a aria-label="Link to this section" data-heading-link="" href="#automatic-caching"><span><svg viewBox="0 0 16 16" height="0.7em" width="0.7em"><g stroke-width="1.2" fill="none" stroke="currentColor"><path fill="none" stroke-linecap="round" stroke-linejoin="round" stroke-miterlimit="10" d="M8.995,7.005 L8.995,7.005c1.374,1.374,1.374,3.601,0,4.975l-1.99,1.99c-1.374,1.374-3.601,1.374-4.975,0l0,0c-1.374-1.374-1.374-3.601,0-4.975 l1.748-1.698"></path><path fill="none" stroke-linecap="round" stroke-linejoin="round" stroke-miterlimit="10" d="M7.005,8.995 L7.005,8.995c-1.374-1.374-1.374-3.601,0-4.975l1.99-1.99c1.374-1.374,3.601-1.374,4.975,0l0,0c1.374,1.374,1.374,3.601,0,4.975 l-1.748,1.698"></path></g></svg></span></a></h3>
<ul>
<li>Next.js sets the <code>Cache-Control</code> header of <code>public, max-age=31536000, immutable</code> to truly immutable assets. It cannot be overridden. These immutable files contain a SHA-hash in the file name, so they can be safely cached indefinitely. For example, <a href="/docs/app/getting-started/images#local-images">Static Image Imports</a>. You can <a href="/docs/app/api-reference/components/image#minimumcachettl">configure the TTL</a> for images.</li>
<li>Incremental Static Regeneration (ISR) sets the <code>Cache-Control</code> header of <code>s-maxage: &lt;revalidate in getStaticProps&gt;, stale-while-revalidate</code>. This revalidation time is defined in your <a href="/docs/pages/building-your-application/data-fetching/get-static-props"><code>getStaticProps</code> function</a> in seconds. If you set <code>revalidate: false</code>, it will default to a one-year cache duration. To leverage this at the CDN layer, your CDN/reverse proxy must respect these directives and cache-key variability (<a href="/docs/app/guides/cdn-caching">CDN Caching</a>); otherwise, responses may bypass CDN caching or serve stale/mismatched variants during client-side navigation.</li>
<li>Dynamically rendered pages set a <code>Cache-Control</code> header of <code>private, no-cache, no-store, max-age=0, must-revalidate</code> to prevent user-specific data from being cached. This applies to both the App Router and Pages Router. This also includes <a href="/docs/app/guides/draft-mode">Draft Mode</a>.</li>
</ul>
<h3 id="static-assets" data-docs-heading="">Static Assets<a aria-label="Link to this section" data-heading-link="" href="#static-assets"><span><svg viewBox="0 0 16 16" height="0.7em" width="0.7em"><g stroke-width="1.2" fill="none" stroke="currentColor"><path fill="none" stroke-linecap="round" stroke-linejoin="round" stroke-miterlimit="10" d="M8.995,7.005 L8.995,7.005c1.374,1.374,1.374,3.601,0,4.975l-1.99,1.99c-1.374,1.374-3.601,1.374-4.975,0l0,0c-1.374-1.374-1.374-3.601,0-4.975 l1.748-1.698"></path><path fill="none" stroke-linecap="round" stroke-linejoin="round" stroke-miterlimit="10" d="M7.005,8.995 L7.005,8.995c-1.374-1.374-1.374-3.601,0-4.975l1.99-1.99c1.374-1.374,3.601-1.374,4.975,0l0,0c1.374,1.374,1.374,3.601,0,4.975 l-1.748,1.698"></path></g></svg></span></a></h3>
<p>If you want to host static assets on a different domain or CDN, you can use the <code>assetPrefix</code> <a href="/docs/app/api-reference/config/next-config-js/assetPrefix">configuration</a> in <code>next.config.js</code>. Next.js will use this asset prefix when retrieving JavaScript or CSS files. Separating your assets to a different domain does come with the downside of extra time spent on DNS and TLS resolution.</p>
<p><a href="/docs/app/api-reference/config/next-config-js/assetPrefix">Learn more about <code>assetPrefix</code></a>.</p>
<h3 id="configuring-caching" data-docs-heading="">Configuring Caching<a aria-label="Link to this section" data-heading-link="" href="#configuring-caching"><span><svg viewBox="0 0 16 16" height="0.7em" width="0.7em"><g stroke-width="1.2" fill="none" stroke="currentColor"><path fill="none" stroke-linecap="round" stroke-linejoin="round" stroke-miterlimit="10" d="M8.995,7.005 L8.995,7.005c1.374,1.374,1.374,3.601,0,4.975l-1.99,1.99c-1.374,1.374-3.601,1.374-4.975,0l0,0c-1.374-1.374-1.374-3.601,0-4.975 l1.748-1.698"></path><path fill="none" stroke-linecap="round" stroke-linejoin="round" stroke-miterlimit="10" d="M7.005,8.995 L7.005,8.995c-1.374-1.374-1.374-3.601,0-4.975l1.99-1.99c1.374-1.374,3.601-1.374,4.975,0l0,0c1.374,1.374,1.374,3.601,0,4.975 l-1.748,1.698"></path></g></svg></span></a></h3>
<p>By default, generated cache assets will be stored in memory (defaults to 50mb) and on disk. On ephemeral compute platforms (common serverless setups), local disk is often non-persistent or unavailable, so this cache is effectively short-lived and per-instance. If you are hosting Next.js using a container orchestration platform like Kubernetes, each pod will have a copy of the cache. To prevent stale data from being shown since the cache is not shared between pods by default, you can configure the Next.js cache to provide a cache handler and disable in-memory caching.</p>
<p>To configure the cache location when self-hosting, you can configure a custom handler in your <code>next.config.js</code> file:</p>
<p>For production deployments, use this as a starting point and extend it with durable storage, eviction policies, error handling, and distributed tag coordination. See <a href="/docs/app/api-reference/config/next-config-js/incrementalCacheHandlerPath">Custom Next.js Cache Handler</a> and the <a href="https://github.com/vercel/next.js/tree/canary/examples/cache-handler-redis" rel="noopener noreferrer" target="_blank">Redis <code>cacheHandler</code> example<span class="inline-flex not-prose"><svg viewBox="0 0 16 16" height="16" width="16" data-slot="geist-icon" style="color:currentColor"><path fill="currentColor" fill-rule="evenodd" d="M6.75 4H6v1.5h3.44L5.47 9.47l-.53.53L6 11.06l.53-.53 3.97-3.97V10H12V5a1 1 0 0 0-1-1z" clip-rule="evenodd"></path></svg></span></a>. If you are configuring backends for <code>&#x27;use cache&#x27;</code> directives, use <a href="/docs/app/api-reference/config/next-config-js/cacheHandlers"><code>cacheHandlers</code></a>.</p>

<p>Using a custom cache handler will allow you to ensure consistency across all pods hosting your Next.js application. For instance, you can save the cached values anywhere, like <a href="https://github.com/vercel/next.js/tree/canary/examples/cache-handler-redis" rel="noopener noreferrer" target="_blank">Redis<span class="inline-flex not-prose"><svg viewBox="0 0 16 16" height="16" width="16" data-slot="geist-icon" style="color:currentColor"><path fill="currentColor" fill-rule="evenodd" d="M6.75 4H6v1.5h3.44L5.47 9.47l-.53.53L6 11.06l.53-.53 3.97-3.97V10H12V5a1 1 0 0 0-1-1z" clip-rule="evenodd"></path></svg></span></a> or AWS S3.</p>
<blockquote class="p-3 text-sm">
<p><strong>Good to know:</strong></p>
<ul>
<li><code>revalidatePath</code> is a convenience layer on top of cache tags. Calling <code>revalidatePath</code> will call the <code>revalidateTag</code> function with a special default tag for the provided page.</li>
</ul>
</blockquote>
<h2 id="build-cache" data-docs-heading="">Build Cache<a aria-label="Link to this section" data-heading-link="" href="#build-cache"><span><svg viewBox="0 0 16 16" height="0.7em" width="0.7em"><g stroke-width="1.2" fill="none" stroke="currentColor"><path fill="none" stroke-linecap="round" stroke-linejoin="round" stroke-miterlimit="10" d="M8.995,7.005 L8.995,7.005c1.374,1.374,1.374,3.601,0,4.975l-1.99,1.99c-1.374,1.374-3.601,1.374-4.975,0l0,0c-1.374-1.374-1.374-3.601,0-4.975 l1.748-1.698"></path><path fill="none" stroke-linecap="round" stroke-linejoin="round" stroke-miterlimit="10" d="M7.005,8.995 L7.005,8.995c-1.374-1.374-1.374-3.601,0-4.975l1.99-1.99c1.374-1.374,3.601-1.374,4.975,0l0,0c1.374,1.374,1.374,3.601,0,4.975 l-1.748,1.698"></path></g></svg></span></a></h2>
<p>Next.js generates an ID during <code>next build</code> to identify which version of your application is being served. The same build should be used and boot up multiple containers.</p>
<p>If you are rebuilding for each stage of your environment, you will need to generate a consistent build ID to use between containers. Use the <code>generateBuildId</code> command in <code>next.config.js</code>:</p>

<blockquote class="p-3 text-sm">
<p><strong>Good to know:</strong> When <a href="/docs/app/api-reference/config/next-config-js/deploymentId"><code>deploymentId</code></a> is set, Next.js uses a constant build ID and <code>generateBuildId</code> has no effect. <a href="#version-skew">Version skew</a> is detected from the deployment ID instead.</p>
</blockquote>
<h2 id="multi-server-deployments" data-docs-heading="">Multi-Server Deployments<a aria-label="Link to this section" data-heading-link="" href="#multi-server-deployments"><span><svg viewBox="0 0 16 16" height="0.7em" width="0.7em"><g stroke-width="1.2" fill="none" stroke="currentColor"><path fill="none" stroke-linecap="round" stroke-linejoin="round" stroke-miterlimit="10" d="M8.995,7.005 L8.995,7.005c1.374,1.374,1.374,3.601,0,4.975l-1.99,1.99c-1.374,1.374-3.601,1.374-4.975,0l0,0c-1.374-1.374-1.374-3.601,0-4.975 l1.748-1.698"></path><path fill="none" stroke-linecap="round" stroke-linejoin="round" stroke-miterlimit="10" d="M7.005,8.995 L7.005,8.995c-1.374-1.374-1.374-3.601,0-4.975l1.99-1.99c1.374-1.374,3.601-1.374,4.975,0l0,0c1.374,1.374,1.374,3.601,0,4.975 l-1.748,1.698"></path></g></svg></span></a></h2>
<p>When running Next.js across multiple server instances (for example, containers behind a load balancer), there are additional considerations to ensure consistent behavior.</p>
<h3 id="server-functions-encryption-key" data-docs-heading="">Server Functions encryption key<a aria-label="Link to this section" data-heading-link="" href="#server-functions-encryption-key"><span><svg viewBox="0 0 16 16" height="0.7em" width="0.7em"><g stroke-width="1.2" fill="none" stroke="currentColor"><path fill="none" stroke-linecap="round" stroke-linejoin="round" stroke-miterlimit="10" d="M8.995,7.005 L8.995,7.005c1.374,1.374,1.374,3.601,0,4.975l-1.99,1.99c-1.374,1.374-3.601,1.374-4.975,0l0,0c-1.374-1.374-1.374-3.601,0-4.975 l1.748-1.698"></path><path fill="none" stroke-linecap="round" stroke-linejoin="round" stroke-miterlimit="10" d="M7.005,8.995 L7.005,8.995c-1.374-1.374-1.374-3.601,0-4.975l1.99-1.99c1.374-1.374,3.601-1.374,4.975,0l0,0c1.374,1.374,1.374,3.601,0,4.975 l-1.748,1.698"></path></g></svg></span></a></h3>
<p>Next.js encrypts <a href="/docs/app/getting-started/mutating-data">Server Function</a> closure variables before sending them to the client. By default, a unique encryption key is generated for each build.</p>
<p>When running multiple server instances, all instances must use the same encryption key. Otherwise, a Server Function encrypted by one instance cannot be decrypted by another, causing &quot;Failed to find Server Action&quot; errors.</p>
<p>Set a consistent encryption key using the <code>NEXT_SERVER_ACTIONS_ENCRYPTION_KEY</code> environment variable. The key must be a base64-encoded value with a valid AES key length (16, 24, or 32 bytes). Next.js generates 32-byte keys by default.</p>

.25.11.25.25v7.5q-.02.23-.25.25h-5.5a.25.25 0 0 1-.25-.25v-7.5c0-.14.11-.25.25-.25zm0 9.5c.97 0 1.75-.78 1.75-1.75v-7.5c0-.97-.78-1.75-1.75-1.75h-5.5C6.78 4.5 6 5.28 6 6.25v7.5c0 .97.78 1.75 1.75 [&amp;&gt;pre]:rounded-t-lg border-t"><pre class="vF3MAa_pre"><code class="font-mono grid text-left !text-[13px] leading-[20px] break-normal hyphens-none whitespace-pre text-[var(--ds-gray-1000)]" style="font-feature-settings:&quot;liga&quot; off"><span class="line" data-line=""><span style="color:var(--shiki-color-text)">NEXT_SERVER_ACTIONS_ENCRYPTION_KEY</span><span style="color:var(--shiki-token-keyword)">=</span><span style="color:var(--shiki-token-string)">your-generated-key</span><span style="color:var(--shiki-token-function)"> next</span><span style="color:var(--shiki-token-string)"> build</span></span></code></pre></div></div>
<p>The key is embedded in the build output and used automatically at runtime. Learn more in the <a href="/docs/app/guides/data-security#overwriting-encryption-keys-advanced">Data Security guide</a>.</p>
<h3 id="deployment-identifier" data-docs-heading="">Deployment identifier<a aria-label="Link to this section" data-heading-link="" href="#deployment-identifier"><span><svg viewBox="0 0 16 16" height="0.7em" width="0.7em"><g stroke-width="1.2" fill="none" stroke="currentColor"><path fill="none" stroke-linecap="round" stroke-linejoin="round" stroke-miterlimit="10" d="M8.995,7.005 L8.995,7.005c1.374,1.374,1.374,3.601,0,4.975l-1.99,1.99c-1.374,1.374-3.601,1.374-4.975,0l0,0c-1.374-1.374-1.374-3.601,0-4.975 l1.748-1.698"></path><path fill="none" stroke-linecap="round" stroke-linejoin="round" stroke-miterlimit="10" d="M7.005,8.995 L7.005,8.995c-1.374-1.374-1.374-3.601,0-4.975l1.99-1.99c1.374-1.374,3.601-1.374,4.975,0l0,0c1.374,1.374,1.374,3.601,0,4.975 l-1.748,1.698"></path></g></svg></span></a></h3>
<p>Configure a <a href="/docs/app/api-reference/config/next-config-js/deploymentId"><code>deploymentId</code></a> to enable version skew protection during rolling deployments. This ensures clients always receive assets from a consistent deployment version.</p>
<h3 id="shared-cache" data-docs-heading="">Shared cache<a aria-label="Link to this section" data-heading-link="" href="#shared-cache"><span><svg viewBox="0 0 16 16" height="0.7em" width="0.7em"><g stroke-width="1.2" fill="none" stroke="currentColor"><path fill="none" stroke-linecap="round" stroke-linejoin="round" stroke-miterlimit="10" d="M8.995,7.005 L8.995,7.005c1.374,1.374,1.374,3.601,0,4.975l-1.99,1.99c-1.374,1.374-3.601,1.374-4.975,0l0,0c-1.374-1.374-1.374-3.601,0-4.975 l1.748-1.698"></path><path fill="none" stroke-linecap="round" stroke-linejoin="round" stroke-miterlimit="10" d="M7.005,8.995 L7.005,8.995c-1.374-1.374-1.374-3.601,0-4.975l1.99-1.99c1.374-1.374,3.601-1.374,4.975,0l0,0c1.374,1.374,1.374,3.601,0,4.975 l-1.748,1.698"></path></g></svg></span></a></h3>
<p>By default, Next.js uses an in-memory cache that is not shared across instances. For consistent caching behavior, use <a href="/docs/app/api-reference/directives/use-cache-remote"><code>&#x27;use cache: remote&#x27;</code></a> with a <a href="/docs/app/api-reference/config/next-config-js/cacheHandlers">custom cache handler</a> that stores data in external storage.</p>
<h2 id="version-skew" data-docs-heading="">Version Skew<a aria-label="Link to this section" data-heading-link="" href="#version-skew"><span><svg viewBox="0 0 16 16" height="0.7em" width="0.7em"><g stroke-width="1.2" fill="none" stroke="currentColor"><path fill="none" stroke-linecap="round" stroke-linejoin="round" stroke-miterlimit="10" d="M8.995,7.005 L8.995,7.005c1.374,1.374,1.374,3.601,0,4.975l-1.99,1.99c-1.374,1.374-3.601,1.374-4.975,0l0,0c-1.374-1.374-1.374-3.601,0-4.975 l1.748-1.698"></path><path fill="none" stroke-linecap="round" stroke-linejoin="round" stroke-miterlimit="10" d="M7.005,8.995 L7.005,8.995c-1.374-1.374-1.374-3.601,0-4.975l1.99-1.99c1.374-1.374,3.601-1.374,4.975,0l0,0c1.374,1.374,1.374,3.601,0,4.975 l-1.748,1.698"></path></g></svg></span></a></h2>
<p>When self-hosting across multiple instances or doing rolling deployments, <a href="/docs/app/glossary#version-skew">version skew</a> can cause:</p>
<ul>
<li><strong>Missing assets</strong>: The client requests JavaScript or CSS files that no longer exist on the server</li>
<li><strong>Server Function mismatches</strong>: The client invokes a Server Function using an ID from a previous build that the server no longer recognizes</li>
<li><strong>Navigation failures</strong>: Prefetched page data from an old deployment is incompatible with the new server</li>
</ul>
<p>Next.js uses the <a href="/docs/app/api-reference/config/next-config-js/deploymentId"><code>deploymentId</code></a> to detect and handle version skew. When a deployment ID is configured:</p>
<ul>
<li>Static assets include a <code>?dpl=&lt;deploymentId&gt;</code> query parameter</li>
<li>Client-side navigation requests include an <code>x-deployment-id</code> header</li>
<li>The server compares the client&#x27;s deployment ID with its own</li>
</ul>
<p>If a mismatch is detected, Next.js triggers a hard navigation (full page reload) instead of a client-side navigation. This ensures the client fetches assets from a consistent deployment version.</p>

</blockquote>
<h2 id="streaming-and-suspense" data-docs-heading="">Streaming and Suspense<a aria-label="Link to this section" data-heading-link="" href="#streaming-and-suspense"><span><svg viewBox="0 0 16 16" height="0.7em" width="0.7em"><g stroke-width="1.2" fill="none" stroke="currentColor"><path fill="none" stroke-linecap="round" stroke-linejoin="round" stroke-miterlimit="10" d="M8.995,7.005 L8.995,7.005c1.374,1.374,1.374,3.601,0,4.975l-1.99,1.99c-1.374,1.374-3.601,1.374-4.975,0l0,0c-1.374-1.374-1.374-3.601,0-4.975 l1.748-1.698"></path><path fill="none" stroke-linecap="round" stroke-linejoin="round" stroke-miterlimit="10" d="M7.005,8.995 L7.005,8.995c-1.374-1.374-1.374-3.601,0-4.975l1.99-1.99c1.374-1.374,3.601-1.374,4.975,0l0,0c1.374,1.374,1.374,3.601,0,4.975 l-1.748,1.698"></path></g></svg></span></a></h2><p>The Next.js App Router supports <a href="/docs/app/api-reference/file-conventions/loading">streaming responses</a> when self-hosting. If you are using nginx or a similar proxy, you will need to configure it to disable buffering to enable streaming.</p><p>For example, you can disable buffering in nginx by setting <code>X-Accel-Buffering</code> to <code>no</code>:</p><div class="my-4 rounded-lg group/copy relative overflow-hidden rounded-b-lg not-prose" data-agent-content="code" data-section="content" class="border-gray-400 border-x rounded-b-lg [&amp;&gt;pre]:rounded-b-lg border-b"><pre class="vF3MAa_pre"><code class="font-mono grid text-left !text-[13px] leading-[20px] break-normal hyphens-none whitespace-pre text-[var(--ds-gray-1000)]" style="font-feature-settings:&quot;liga&quot; off"><span class="line" data-line=""><span style="color:var(--shiki-token-constant)">module</span><span style="color:var(--shiki-color-text)">.</span><span style="color:var(--shiki-token-constant)">exports</span><span style="color:var(--shiki-token-keyword)"> =</span><span style="color:var(--shiki-color-text)"> {</span></span>
<span class="line" data-line=""><span style="color:var(--shiki-token-keyword)">  async</span><span style="color:var(--shiki-token-function)"> headers</span><span style="color:var(--shiki-color-text)">() {</span></span>
<span class="line" data-line=""><span style="color:var(--shiki-token-keyword)">    return</span><span style="color:var(--shiki-color-text)"> [</span></span>
<span class="line" data-line=""><span style="color:var(--shiki-color-text)">      {</span></span>
<span class="line" data-line=""><span style="color:var(--shiki-color-text)">        source</span><span style="color:var(--shiki-token-keyword)">:</span><span style="color:var(--shiki-token-string-expression)"> &#x27;/:path*{/}?&#x27;</span><span style="color:var(--shiki-token-punctuation)">,</span></span>
<span class="line" data-line=""><span style="color:var(--shiki-color-text)">        headers</span><span style="color:var(--shiki-token-keyword)">:</span><span style="color:var(--shiki-color-text)"> [</span></span>
<span class="line" data-line=""><span style="color:var(--shiki-color-text)">          {</span></span>
<span class="line" data-line=""><span style="color:var(--shiki-color-text)">            key</span><span style="color:var(--shiki-token-keyword)">:</span><span style="color:var(--shiki-token-string-expression)"> &#x27;X-Accel-Buffering&#x27;</span><span style="color:var(--shiki-token-punctuation)">,</span></span>
<span class="line" data-line=""><span style="color:var(--shiki-color-text)">            value</span><span style="color:var(--shiki-token-keyword)">:</span><span style="color:var(--shiki-token-string-expression)"> &#x27;no&#x27;</span><span style="color:var(--shiki-token-punctuation)">,</span></span>
<span class="line" data-line=""><span style="color:var(--shiki-color-text)">          }</span><span style="color:var(--shiki-token-punctuation)">,</span></span>
<span class="line" data-line=""><span style="color:var(--shiki-color-text)">        ]</span><span style="color:var(--shiki-token-punctuation)">,</span></span>
<span class="line" data-line=""><span style="color:var(--shiki-color-text)">      }</span><span style="color:var(--shiki-token-punctuation)">,</span></span>
<span class="line" data-line=""><span style="color:var(--shiki-color-text)">    ]</span></span>
<span class="line" data-line=""><span style="color:var(--shiki-color-text)">  }</span><span style="color:var(--shiki-token-punctuation)">,</span></span>
<span class="line" data-line=""><span style="color:var(--shiki-color-text)">}</span></span></code></pre></div></div><p>Beyond nginx, ensure that your entire infrastructure supports streaming end-to-end:</p><ul>
<li><strong>Load balancers</strong> must support chunked transfer encoding or HTTP/2 streaming. Some cloud load balancers (for example, AWS ALB with Lambda integration) may buffer responses by default.</li>
<li><strong>Reverse proxies</strong> between the load balancer and Next.js must also pass through chunked responses without buffering.</li>
<li>If using <a href="/docs/app/guides/ppr-platform-guide">Partial Prerendering</a>, streaming support is required. Without it, the static shell and dynamic content are delivered together after the full render completes, eliminating PPR&#x27;s time-to-first-byte advantage.</li>
</ul><h2 id="multi-instance-cache-coordination" data-docs-heading="">Multi-Instance Cache Coordination<a aria-label="Link to this section" data-heading-link="" href="#multi-instance-cache-coordination"><span><svg viewBox="0 0 16 16" height="0.7em" width="0.7em"><g stroke-width="1.2" fill="none" stroke="currentColor"><path fill="none" stroke-linecap="round" stroke-linejoin="round" stroke-miterlimit="10" d="M8.995,7.005 L8.995,7.005c1.374,1.374,1.374,3.601,0,4.975l-1.99,1.99c-1.374,1.374-3.601,1.374-4.975,0l0,0c-1.374-1.374-1.374-3.601,0-4.975 l1.748-1.698"></path><path fill="none" stroke-linecap="round" stroke-linejoin="round" stroke-miterlimit="10" d="M7.005,8.995 L7.005,8.995c-1.374-1.374-1.374-3.601,0-4.975l1.99-1.99c1.374-1.374,3.601-1.374,4.975,0l0,0c1.374,1.374,1.374,3.601,0,4.975 l-1.748,1.698"></path></g></svg></span></a></h2><p>In addition to the <a href="/docs/app/guides/self-hosting#multi-server-deployments">multi-server configuration</a> above (encryption key, deployment ID, shared cache), App Router deployments with multiple instances need cache tag coordination.</p><p>By default, calling <a href="/docs/app/api-reference/functions/revalidateTag"><code>revalidateTag()</code></a> on one instance only invalidates the cache on that instance. Other instances continue serving stale content until they independently discover the invalidation.</p><p>To coordinate tag invalidation across instances, implement the <a href="/docs/app/api-reference/config/next-config-js/cacheHandlers#refreshtags"><code>refreshTags()</code></a> method in your <a href="/docs/app/api-reference/config/next-config-js/cacheHandlers">custom cache handler</a>. This method is called before each request and should sync tag state from shared storage (like Redis) so all instances learn about invalidations promptly.</p><p>For a detailed explanation of the tag architecture, see <a href="/docs/app/guides/how-revalidation-works">How Revalidation Works</a>.</p><h2 id="cache-components" data-docs-heading="">Cache Components<a aria-label="Link to this section" data-heading-link="" href="#cache-components"><span><svg viewBox="0 0 16 16" height="0.7em" width="0.7em"><g stroke-width="1.2" fill="none" stroke="currentColor"><path fill="none" stroke-linecap="round" stroke-linejoin="round" stroke-miterlimit="10" d="M8.995,7.005 L8.995,7.005c1.374,1.374,1.374,3.601,0,4.975l-1.99,1.99c-1.374,1.374-3.601,1.374-4.975,0l0,0c-1.374-1.374-1.374-3.601,0-4.975 l1.748-1.698"></path><path fill="none" stroke-linecap="round" stroke-linejoin="round" stroke-miterlimit="10" d="M7.005,8.995 L7.005,8.995c-1.374-1.374-1.374-3.601,0-4.975l1.99-1.99c1.374-1.374,3.601-1.374,4.975,0l0,0c1.374,1.374,1.374,3.601,0,4.975 l-1.748,1.698"></path></g></svg></span></a></h2><p><a href="/docs/app/getting-started/caching">Cache Components</a> works by default with Next.js and is not a CDN-only feature. This includes deployment as a Node.js server (through <code>next start</code>) and when used with a Docker container.</p><h2 id="usage-with-cdns" data-docs-heading="">Usage with CDNs<a aria-label="Link to this section" data-heading-link="" href="#usage-with-cdns"><span><svg viewBox="0 0 16 16" height="0.7em" width="0.7em"><g stroke-width="1.2" fill="none" stroke="currentColor"><path fill="none" stroke-linecap="round" stroke-linejoin="round" stroke-miterlimit="10" d="M8.995,7.005 L8.995,7.005c1.374,1.374,1.374,3.601,0,4.975l-1.99,1.99c-1.374,1.374-3.601,1.374-4.975,0l0,0c-1.374-1.374-1.374-3.601,0-4.975 l1.748-1.698"></path><path fill="none" stroke-linecap="round" stroke-linejoin="round" stroke-miterlimit="10" d="M7.005,8.995 L7.005,8.995c-1.374-1.374-1.374-3.601,0-4.975l1.99-1.99c1.374-1.374,3.601-1.374,4.975,0l0,0c1.374,1.374,1.374,3.601,0,4.975 l-1.748,1.698"></path></g></svg></span></a></h2><p>When using a CDN in front of your Next.js application, the page will include <code>Cache-Control: private</code> response header when dynamic APIs are accessed. This ensures that the resulting HTML page is marked as non-cacheable. If the page is fully prerendered to static, it will include <code>Cache-Control: public</code> to allow the page to be cached on the CDN.</p><p>If you don&#x27;t need a mix of both static and dynamic components, you can make your entire route static and cache the output HTML on a CDN. This Automatic Static Optimization is the default behavior when running <code>next build</code> if dynamic APIs are not used.</p><p>For detailed guidance on CDN caching behavior, graceful degradation, and cache variability, see <a href="/docs/app/guides/cdn-caching">CDN Caching</a>. For Partial Prerendering support on different platforms, see the <a href="/docs/app/guides/ppr-platform-guide">PPR Platform Guide</a> and the <a href="/docs/app/api-reference/config/next-config-js/adapterPath">Deployment Adapter API</a>.</p>
<h2 id="after" data-docs-heading=""><code>after</code><a aria-label="Link to this section" data-heading-link="" href="#after"><span><svg viewBox="0 0 16 16" height="0.7em" width="0.7em"><g stroke-width="1.2" fill="none" stroke="currentColor"><path fill="none" stroke-linecap="round" stroke-linejoin="round" stroke-miterlimit="10" d="M8.995,7.005 L8.995,7.005c1.374,1.374,1.374,3.601,0,4.975l-1.99,1.99c-1.374,1.374-3.601,1.374-4.975,0l0,0c-1.374-1.374-1.374-3.601,0-4.975 l1.748-1.698"></path><path fill="none" stroke-linecap="round" stroke-linejoin="round" stroke-miterlimit="10" d="M7.005,8.995 L7.005,8.995c-1.374-1.374-1.374-3.601,0-4.975l1.99-1.99c1.374-1.374,3.601-1.374,4.975,0l0,0c1.374,1.374,1.374,3.601,0,4.975 l-1.748,1.698"></path></g></svg></span></a></h2><p><a href="/docs/app/api-reference/functions/after"><code>after</code></a> is fully supported when self-hosting with <code>next start</code>.</p><p>When stopping the server, ensure a graceful shutdown by sending <code>SIGINT</code> or <code>SIGTERM</code> signals and waiting. The Next.js server will finish in-flight requests and execute any pending <code>after()</code> callbacks before exiting. Platforms should allow a configurable drain period (10-30 seconds is recommended) to ensure all background work completes.</p>
